The schema rejected, the retry at lower temp re-emitted the same shape, ladder exhausted, `[Stage1Shadow] exhausted retry budget after 2 attempt(s)` -- cascade continued without the shadow plan.
- **Cause:** `class Stage1Beat` unconditional field constraint `length_target_words: int = Field(..., ge=5, le=200, ...)`. The `ge=5` floor was right for voiced beats (Stage 3 length validator pin) but wrong for music_inter beats where 0 is the natural value.
- **Fix:** Field relaxed to `ge=0`. New `_voiced_beats_have_min_words` model_validator re-asserts `>= 5` ONLY when `speaker != 'MUSIC'`. Character + announcer beats keep the floor. 6 regression tests in `tests/test_bug_local_282_to_285_quad_fix.py::TestBugLocal282MusicBeatAllowsZeroWords` pin (MUSIC zero accepted / MUSIC nonzero accepted / character zero rejected / announcer zero rejected / character floor=5 still enforced / upper bound 200 unchanged).
- **Verify:** Live-run; observe `[Stage1Plan] attempt 1 status=valid_first_attempt` instead of `attempt N parse_failed`.
- **Tags:** writer, stage1, schema-relaxation, music-beats

### BUG-LOCAL-279: LineComposer length-band undershoot on small budgets [FIXED 2026-05-26 -- Option A landed, pending soak verification]
- **Fix landed 2026-05-26 17:15.** Operator picked Option A. `_word_bands(target_words)` now uses a 0.3x floor instead of 0.5x; the 3-word absolute floor binds first on tiny targets, ceiling unchanged at 1.7x. New band for target=20 is `[6..34]` (was `[10..34]`); the operator soak's actual line outputs (5/8/5 words on REN BLACK) now land inside the band on 2/3 lines instead of 0/3, and the model's natural distribution mode (~6-15 words) sits inside the band on every small-budget smoke target. The 5-line regression suite (`TestBugLocal279SmallBudgetBand` in `tests/test_phase1_composer_prompt.py`) pins the new floor at targets 20, 37, 50; pins the 3-word absolute floor at target=5; pins the ceiling-unchanged invariant across (10, 20, 30, 50, 100). Full OTR suite 3256 / 20 / 0; Bug Bible 23 / 1 / 2xf / 0. Pending verification: a 60-word smoke run after this commit lands should ship character lines in band on first attempt, and the legacy reroll loop should converge instead of exhausting -> needs_full_rerun.

### BUG-LOCAL-279 (original LOGGED investigation, superseded by the FIXED header above):
- **Date:** 2026-05-26 | **Phase:** writer / LineComposer Phase 2C dialogue compose | **Bible candidate:** yes (band-math defaults affect every smoke run + the reroll-loop convergence)
- **Symptom:** Every small-budget Sprint 10A operator soak run (60-110 word total budget, 1-2 characters) trips the LineComposer's final-attempt drift floor. Concrete evidence from `pending_20260526_161845` (60-word, 1-char, REN BLACK, build `256f73c` 16:18 2026-05-26):
    * Line 1: target=20, attempt 1 = 17 words above (37 total -- band [10..34]), attempt 2 = "shipping drifty line on final attempt 2: length drift: 2 words outside band [10..34] for target=20" -> shipped 2 words.
    * Line 2: target=20, attempt 1 retry on length drift, attempt 2 = drifty ship at 8 words.
    * Line 3: target=20, attempt 1 = OK at 10 words, no retry.
  Same shape across runs 1-4 (different episodes, different cast, same drift pattern). The legacy story critic flags the short line as "flat", the Sprint 5C reroll loop runs 2 cycles on the same line, the re-composed line is ALSO short (6-13 words), exhausts, stamps `freeze_verdict=needs_full_rerun`. `pending_20260526_154105` exhibited the same end-to-end ladder (cycle 1: 13 words / cycle 2: 18 words, both still flagged flat by the critic).
- **Cause:** Two compounding factors.
    1. **Budget math** -- writer Phase 2A allocates `per_phase_words=[60]` and splits across `per_phase_beats=[3]` for an effective 20 words/beat target. Mistral-Nemo on the creative slot at temp=0.85 / 0.92 (creativity=balanced / wild&rough) prefers SHORTER dialogue than 20 words per line -- the natural-output distribution is ~5-15 words/line. When the prompt asks for "around 20 words for this beat", the model emits a tight 5-8 word line that's still semantically valid but fails the band check.
    2. **Band floor too strict on small targets** -- `_word_bands(target_words=20)` returns `min_words=max(3, int(20*0.5))=10`, `max_words=max(11, int(20*1.7))=34`. The 50% floor scales linearly; on target=20 the model needs to hit at least 10 words, which is exactly the floor it misses. On larger targets (target=37, the 110-word per-line floor) the band is [18..62] -- still missed in the run-3 log (8 + 11 + 7 words shipped, 4 of 5 sub-floor).
- **Fix shape (three options, operator decision pending):**
    1. **(A) Lower the min floor on small targets.** `min_words = max(3, int(target * 0.4))` for target <= 25, keep `0.5` floor for 26+. On target=20 this drops the floor from 10 to 8; on target=37 the band stays [18..62] (unchanged from current). 5-line cost. Preserves band-tightness on normal-sized episodes, just relaxes it on the small-budget smoke surface.
    2. **(B) Change beat allocation.** Writer Phase 2A currently splits 60 words / 3 character beats = 20/beat. Switch to fewer-but-longer-lines bias: 60 words / 2 char beats × 30 words/beat. Each line has more headroom to converge, the model's natural ~5-15 word distribution falls inside [15..51] easily. This is a Phase 2A budget refactor, larger change.
    3. **(C) Prompt-level nudge in `_otr_line_composer._build_user_prompt`.** Add "Aim for at least N words" instruction tied to `min_words`. May help the model converge but is "hope the model listens" vs the deterministic fix of (A).
  Recommendation if I had to pick: **A**. It's the smallest fix that addresses the actual symptom (the model is right, the band is wrong on this scale); it preserves behavior on normal-sized episodes (where the band tightness has been load-bearing for years); and it's testable in unit. (B) is the better long-term answer but requires re-thinking the writer's budget allocation. (C) is hope-driven.
- **Verify (post-fix):** soak run with 60-word budget + Option A landed -> assert `compose_flags` does NOT carry `length_drift` warnings, the legacy critic loop converges (no `needs_full_rerun`), and Bark renders without the BUG-LOCAL-276 halt firing.
- **Non-blocking on Sprint 10A code-completeness:** the legacy critic loop is independent of Sprint 10A (`enable_stage1_shadow_pass=True` does not affect the LineComposer). The `bypass_freeze_halt` widget on Bark is the smoke-iteration escape hatch -- with it ON, audio + video ship despite the critic loop's pessimistic verdict.
- **Tags:** linecomposer, length-band, drift, budget-math, smoke-iteration, sprint-5c-reroll, bug-local-276-coupled

### BUG-LOCAL-278: Freeze cascade meta modifications not persisted to disk at exit points [FIXED 2026-05-26 -- pending soak verification]
- **Date:** 2026-05-26 | **Phase:** freeze cascade (architectural -- predates Sprint 10A) | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Sprint 10A operator soak run `signal_lost_..._154105`. The live cascade log shows `meta.freeze_verdict='needs_full_rerun'`, `meta.story_critic_report` (with real `arc_verdict=uneven` + reroll targets), and `meta.stage7_shadow_critic` (with `verdict=discard mean=2.60 failing_axes=[...]`) all stamped. `BatchBarkGenerator` saw `meta.freeze_verdict` correctly and BUG-LOCAL-276's halt fired as designed. But after the run, inspection of the persisted `pending_20260526_154105_ledger.json` showed ALL of those keys ABSENT -- `meta.get('freeze_verdict')` is None, `'stage7_shadow_critic' in meta` is False, `'story_critic_report' in meta` is False. The operator cannot verify Step 8 gate criteria from the .json after the run; forensic tools see a stale ledger; the cascade's audit trail does not persist.
- **Cause:** The freeze cascade modifies `meta` in place at every exit (`freeze_verdict`, `freeze_disposition`, `story_critic_report`, `stage7_shadow_critic`, `freeze_phase_telemetry`, the entire `cleanup_passes` phase log) but does NOT save the ledger to disk on the terminal-skip / Phase-10-reject / successful-freeze exits. Three of the four cascade exits (every exit except the BUG-LOCAL-274 render-plan-persistence path added 2026-05-25) returned without an `led.save()` call. In-memory readers (`BatchBarkGenerator`, `MusicGenTheme`, socket-fed downstream nodes) SEE the cascade stamps because they read the live `led.data` reference from the same in-memory object. Disk readers DON'T: `KokoroAnnouncer.generate` does `_OTRL.load_ledger_safe(ledger_path)` to read the pre-cascade ledger from disk, stamps its own line_id timing fields, and writes back via `save_ledger_safe`. The cascade's modifications are not in that read-from-disk snapshot, so the write-back silently overwrites the disk file with stale state. Pre-existing architectural gap; predates Sprint 10A by months (BUG-LOCAL-274 ack'd the same shape at ONE exit -- the render-plan stamp -- and added a single `_save_fn()` call after that stamp; the other three exits stayed unfixed because no consumer was reading from disk at those points until Sprint 10A step 7's whole-episode critic created a disk-readable diagnostic that the operator's Step 8 gate criterion #4 expected to find).
- **Fix:** [FIXED 2026-05-26] New `_persist_cascade_meta(led)` helper at the top of `nodes/_otr_freeze_cascade.py`, immediately above `_build_terminal_skip_disposition`. The helper does a defensive `getattr(led, "save", None)` + try/except so test fixtures stubbing `led` as a SimpleNamespace without a save method don't crash the cascade, and so production `Ledger.save()` failures (disk full, permission, etc.) log a warning and let the cascade return cleanly -- PD1 ("audio is king") never broken. Wired the helper into every cascade exit:
    1. Reviewer-terminal exit (line ~716, fires when `review_ledger` returns `too_many_edits` / `needs_full_rerun`).
    2. Sprint 5C reroll-exhaustion terminal exit (line ~885, fires when the targeted-reroll loop exhausts cycles with the critic still flagging targets -- the path that fired in the live run).
    3. Phase 10 rejection exit (line ~1024, fires when `phase_10_gap_audit_post_and_freeze` raises `FreezeAssertionError`).
    4. Successful freeze exit (line ~1066, the happy path: `frozen_clean` / `frozen_with_warns` / `frozen_with_doctor_edits`).
    The BUG-LOCAL-274 render-plan-persistence save (line ~870) is preserved as-is; it now runs in addition to the new exit-point save (idempotent -- two saves at the same site flush identical state). Each new save site carries a comment naming BUG-LOCAL-278 and pointing at the helper.
- **Verify:** +5 regression tests in `tests/test_freeze_cascade_meta_persistence.py::TestBug278CascadeMetaPersistedAtEveryExit`:
    * `test_save_fires_on_reroll_exhaustion_exit` -- spy-patches `_persist_cascade_meta`, asserts >=1 call from the reroll-exhaustion path with `RerollDisposition(verdict='needs_full_rerun', ...)` patched in.
    * `test_save_fires_on_successful_freeze_exit` -- advisory-only critic + clean reviewer -> success path, asserts save count > 1 (one for BUG-LOCAL-274's render_plan save, one for the new success-exit save) and last snapshot carries a `frozen_*` verdict.
    * `test_persist_helper_never_raises_on_save_failure` -- subclass of `_RebindingLedger` whose `save()` raises `IOError`; cascade returns cleanly, freeze_verdict on in-memory meta intact, no exception escapes (PD1 pin).
    * `test_persist_helper_handles_simplenamespace_fixture` -- SimpleNamespace `led` without a `save` method; cascade does not crash; in-memory state correct.
    * `test_persist_helper_is_called_at_terminal_reviewer_exit` -- spy-patches the helper, fake-review returns `needs_full_rerun` to hit the terminal-reviewer exit, asserts exactly 1 call from that path (the cascade returns before reaching any other exit).
    The spy-patch approach decouples the BUG-278 wiring check from the BUG-LOCAL-273-family stale-meta-after-reviewer-rebind concern (a stamp on a stale `meta` reference would result in an empty persisted meta even with the fix; that pre-existing dynamic is covered by `TestBug273CriticStampSurvivesReviewerSave` end-to-end). Full OTR suite 3251 passed / 20 skipped / 0 failed (was 3246 + 5 new tests). Bug Bible 23 passed / 1 skipped / 2 xfailed / 0 failed.
- **Tags:** freeze-cascade, persistence, disk-write, kokoro, sprint-10a-step-8, gate-criteria, architectural, pd1-preserved
- **Bible candidate:** yes (the in-memory vs on-disk diagnostic-persistence shape is a recurring footgun across every downstream consumer of `meta.*` diagnostics, and the fix pattern (`_persist_cascade_meta` helper called at every cascade exit) is the canonical answer)

### BUG-LOCAL-277: Stage 7 shadow critic crashes at setup with AttributeError on dict-shaped meta.news_seed [FIXED 2026-05-26 -- pending soak verification]
- **Date:** 2026-05-26 | **Phase:** Sprint 10A step 7 shadow critic / legacy-to-stage1 adapter | **Bible candidate:** no (Sprint 10A internal, dies in catch-all)
- **Symptom:** Live run `signal_lost_..._154105` (HEAD `f8bb45a`, first run with build `c86fda8` loaded after Desktop restart, target_words=110, creativity=wild & rough, both shadow widgets ON). Cascade log line 15:44:46: `[Stage7Shadow] shadow critic raised AttributeError: 'dict' object has no attribute 'strip'; stamping marker and continuing.` The catch-all in `_otr_freeze_cascade.py` did exactly what it was designed to do -- stamped a `shadow_setup_failed` marker into `meta["stage7_shadow_critic"]` and kept the cascade alive. PD1 held; the reroll loop ran, the cascade reached `freeze_verdict=needs_full_rerun` normally, BUG-LOCAL-276's halt fired at Bark as designed. But `meta.stage7_shadow_critic` never carried a real verdict -- the Step 8 gate's criterion #4 (rubric verdict + axis scores present) cannot be checked. Concretely shipped a sprint that the operator can't measure.
- **Cause:** `nodes/_otr_legacy_to_stage1_adapter.py:163` did `news_seed = (meta.get("news_seed") or "").strip()`. The adapter was written assuming `meta.news_seed` is a string -- which it WAS in older / handwritten ledgers. Post-NewsFetcher-refactor (current production code), the field is a dict `{headline, source, url, date, body_chars, style, selected_at}`. `meta.get("news_seed")` returns the dict; `or ""` short-circuits to the dict (truthy); `.strip()` on a dict raises AttributeError. The legacy-string code path was never exercised against a real production ledger before this commit; the unit tests covered the string shape only (`test_stage7_shadow_critic_wiring.py::test_missing_premise_uses_news_seed` uses `led["meta"]["news_seed"] = "Signal anomaly detected..."`).
- **Fix:** New `_coerce_news_seed_text(raw)` helper in `_otr_legacy_to_stage1_adapter.py` -- type-aware: string passes through `.strip()`; dict prefers `headline`, falls back to `source`, then to `""`; anything unexpected returns `""`. Never raises. The adapter now routes `meta.get("news_seed")` through this helper before any `.strip()` call. Pure-Python, no LLM, no schema change. PD1 untouched (shadow critic is gated on `enable_stage1_shadow_pass=True` widget, default False -- zero runtime impact when the operator hasn't opted in). PD6 untouched (no new LLM call site).
- **Verify:** 3 new tests in `tests/test_stage7_shadow_critic_wiring.py`: `test_news_seed_as_dict_does_not_raise` (the exact regression case -- carries the production ledger's full dict shape and asserts `headline` carries through as the premise excerpt); `test_news_seed_dict_falls_back_to_source` (empty headline -> `source` fallback path); `test_news_seed_dict_all_empty_falls_back_to_placeholder` (defensive -- both empty AND episode_canon absent -> placeholder kicks in via the schema floor). The original `test_missing_premise_uses_news_seed` (string shape) is preserved unchanged so both shapes are pinned. Full OTR suite 3266 passed / 20 skipped / 0 failed (was 3262 + 4 new tests counting the broader Sprint 10A growth). Bug Bible regression 23 passed / 1 skipped / 2 xfailed / 0 failed. Repro confirmed from the persisted ledger before/after the fix (pre-fix: `AttributeError: 'dict' object has no attribute 'strip'` at adapter line 163; post-fix: adapter returns a valid `Stage1Plan` with `cast=2, beats=5` from the same ledger).
- **Related (separate bug, NOT fixed here):** BUG-LOCAL-278 surfaces from the same run -- the freeze cascade modifies `meta` in-memory only; Kokoro then reads from disk + writes back, silently dropping the cascade's modifications. `meta.freeze_verdict`, `meta.story_critic_report`, `meta.stage7_shadow_critic`, `meta.freeze_disposition` all in the live in-memory ledger (Bark correctly halts on them) but absent from the persisted ledger. Pre-existing architectural gap; predates Sprint 10A. Tracked separately.
- **Tags:** sprint-10a, step-7, shadow-critic, adapter, attribute-error, type-safety, news-seed, live-run

### BUG-LOCAL-276: Freeze cascade verdict 'needs_full_rerun' silently ignored; Bark crashes downstream on the flagged ledger [HOTFIX LANDED 2026-05-26 -- pending soak verification]
- **Date:** 2026-05-26 | **Phase:** freeze cascade -> Bark TTS (writer cast-lock + freeze-gate enforcement) | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Live run `pending_20260526_053338` ("Algorithms Ascendant"), HEAD `4efd87e`. Sequence in the soak log: `build_news_briefs` failed 3/3 on `key_term exceeds 40 chars` (the NASA/JPL story carries structurally hostile jargon -- "federally funded research and development center (FFRDC)"); writer fell back to raw `news_seed` for cast + outline; cast came up gender-inverted (OSCAR=female on a male-coded name); `[OTR_LedgerReviewer:pre] audit complete: 6 violation(s), pass_clean=False` with `role_mismatch on line_id=b012 suggested expected='bark' not in allowed roles`; reroll exhausted 2 cycles with the critic still naming targets -> `freeze_verdict=needs_full_rerun`. The cascade had done its job and signalled "do not render". The pipeline ignored it: MusicGen ran, Kokoro stamped announcer lines, then `BatchBarkGenerator.generate_batch` at `nodes/batch_bark_generator.py:439` raised `ValueError: BatchBarkGenerator: cast.voice_preset missing or non-v2/* for character 'ANNOUNCER' (line_id=b002, char_id='c01', got 'bm_fable'). Writer cast-lock contract violation (Gate 1 + Gate 2 should have caught this upstream).` Run dead at 325.20 s; no mp4. The Bark error message is technically correct (line `b002` had `role='character'` but `char_id='c01'` resolved to the ANNOUNCER cast row holding a Kokoro voice) but it hides the actual upstream signal -- the freeze cascade had ALREADY flagged the ledger as unrenderable and the pipeline was running on borrowed time.
- **Cause:** Two coupled defects, both upstream of Bark.
    1. **Root cause (writer pipeline architecture, deferred):** the writer's cast-assignment pipeline produced a ledger where line `b002` carries `role='character'` but `char_id='c01'`, where `c01` is the ANNOUNCER row in the cast table (holding `voice_preset='bm_fable'` for Kokoro). Cast-lock contract Gates 1 + 2 (writer + FreezeCascade G6) did not reject this shape. Same defect family as the long-deferred BUG-LOCAL-232; the round-robin synthesis on `docs/2026-05-26-good-story-writer-architecture__00_question.md` is the planned forum to resolve it (Candidate B per-character actor agents may eliminate the cast-assignment surface entirely; Candidate A2 / D may keep it and require Gates 1 + 2 to be re-thought).
    2. **Immediate cause (freeze-gate enforcement, hotfixed by this entry):** the cascade's `meta.freeze_verdict='needs_full_rerun'` had no consumer that halted the pipeline. `OTR_BatchBarkGenerator` only checked the cast-row voice_preset shape at its Gate 3 (`batch_bark_generator.py:438`) -- which is the LAST line of defense, not the FIRST. Filed in 2026-05-19 as the design question BUG-LOCAL-241 ("verdict advisory or blocking?"); the run-3 crash is the concrete recurrence that promotes it from design question to load-bearing bug.
- **Fix:** [HOTFIX LANDED 2026-05-26 -- pending soak verification] for defect #2 only. `nodes/batch_bark_generator.py:generate_batch` now reads `meta.freeze_verdict` immediately after `_OTRLC.load_ledger` and raises a clear `ValueError` when the verdict is `'needs_full_rerun'`: `"BatchBarkGenerator: freeze cascade stamped meta.freeze_verdict='needs_full_rerun' (writer left the ledger in an unrenderable state). Refusing to render TTS on a flagged ledger. Re-run the writer phase; do not bypass this halt. See BUG-LOCAL-276."` Missing verdict (legacy graphs, tests, partial cascades) -> proceed as before; only the explicit `'needs_full_rerun'` literal halts. The halt fires BEFORE any defensive VRAM unload + BEFORE the Gate 3 cast-row check, so the operator sees the actual upstream signal instead of a misleading downstream message. Defect #1 (the writer-pipeline architecture) remains parked for the round-robin synthesis -- if Sprint 10A replaces the cast-assignment surface (Candidate B), this halt becomes a no-op and the cast-row Gate 3 check can also retire; otherwise this halt remains the load-bearing block + Gates 1 + 2 still need their own fix.
- **Verify:** Pending. Acceptance: queue an episode with deliberate cast contract violation injected (or wait for the next NASA/JPL-class jargon-hostile news seed to recur) -- pipeline must halt at the freeze gate with the new `'Refusing to render TTS on a flagged ledger'` message, NEVER reach the old Gate 3 cast-lock crash. Regression: Bug Bible regression + OTR core suite must stay green (run pending; no test exercises freeze_verdict on `BatchBarkGenerator` yet -- a new test that constructs a minimal ledger with `meta.freeze_verdict='needs_full_rerun'` and asserts `generate_batch` raises with the BUG-LOCAL-276 message should land as part of soak verification).
- **Tags:** freeze-cascade, writer, cast-lock, freeze-gate, bark, contract-violation, needs-full-rerun, hotfix, sprint-10a-coupled, bug-local-241-recurrence, live-run

### BUG-LOCAL-275: Script Doctor edits-pass produces null payload, pydantic validation rejects [FIXED 2026-05-25]
- **Date:** 2026-05-25 | **Phase:** freeze cascade / Script Doctor (Sprint 3C surface, exposed by live run) | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Live run `signal_lost_bioluminescent_trench_descent_20260525_182002` on HEAD `244e9fd`. Script Doctor edits pass logged: `[OTR_StructuredCall] 'run_script_doctor_edits' attempt 1 failed: 1 validation error for ScriptDoctorReport / edits.2.payload / Input should be a valid string [type=string_type, input_value=None, input_type=NoneType]`. Attempt 2 (structural retry at 0.3) failed with the identical error. Attempt 3 (typed repair at 0.1) succeeded -- but `_drop_undiagnosed_edits` then deterministically dropped the proposed edits on `b004` + `b014` (the diagnosis had not flagged either line). Net: zero edits applied, doctor degraded safely. Non-blocking; the pipeline continued and the episode shipped.
- **Cause:** The doctor's edit-pass schema requires `payload: str` but the model (`google/gemma-4-E4B-it` on both slots) keeps emitting `payload: null` for some edit rows -- likely status-only / annotation-only edits where the LLM doesn't want to propose new text. The current typed-repair factory in `_otr_repair_prompts.py` does not have a dedicated `payload_null_repair` arm; the structural retry at attempt 2 is just temperature lowering with the same prompt, so the same null gets re-emitted.
- **Fix:** Option (b) shipped 2026-05-25 (Sprint 7C). New `payload_null_repair` typed-prompt factory in `nodes/_otr_repair_prompts.py`. The `make_dispatching_repair_factory` dispatcher detects pydantic ValidationErrors whose lowered repr contains BOTH `payload` (field path) AND `input_value=none` (annotation), and routes them to the new factory BEFORE falling through to the generic `schema_field_repair` (Sprint 2C path preserved for every other ValidationError). The directive is explicit: every `payload` MUST be a non-null replacement string; if the model has no replacement text, OMIT the entire edit row rather than emit `payload: null` / `payload: ""`. Schema stays strict (`ReviewerEdit.payload: str`) -- the recovery is typed, not the schema widening (option (a) was rejected because it would mask intentional doctor mistakes). Detection signal documented in `_PAYLOAD_NULL_FIELD_TOKEN` / `_PAYLOAD_NULL_VALUE_TOKEN` constants; if pydantic ever changes the repr the dispatcher falls through to `schema_field_repair` -- behaviour-degraded, never broken.
- **Verify:** `tests/test_repair_prompts.py` 18 -> 21 (+3): the new builder test (`test_payload_null_repair_directive`), the dispatcher routing test (`test_dispatch_payload_null_routes_to_payload_null_repair`), and the specificity guard (`test_dispatch_payload_null_signal_is_specific` -- a ValidationError on an unrelated null field must NOT take the Sprint 7C route). The specificity guard pins the field-name + null-value paired signal so a future schema with a null `rationale` (or any non-`payload` null) keeps the generic schema_field_repair path. Full OTR suite 2856 passed / 21 skipped / 0 failed (baseline 2853 + 3 new); Bug Bible 16 passed / 7 skipped / 3 xfailed / 0 failed; LLM-slot sweep 23/23 tagged. After fix, the live-run scenario that originally surfaced 275 (attempts 1+2 fail on null payload; attempt 3 recovers but with undiagnosed-line edits that get deterministically dropped) should converge to: attempt 1 fails on null payload, attempt 2 same, attempt 3 typed-repair recovers with the model OMITTING the no-replacement row rather than emitting null -- yielding clean edits the diagnosis already flagged.
- **Tags:** freeze-cascade, script-doctor, structured-call, pydantic, typed-repair, sprint-7c, live-run

### BUG-LOCAL-274: Cascade stamps meta.render_plan in-memory; HuMo reads stale on-disk ledger and ignores the plan [FIXED 2026-05-25]
- **Date:** 2026-05-25 | **Phase:** freeze cascade -> HuMo (Sprint 6 critic -> render coupling) | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Live run `signal_lost_bioluminescent_trench_descent_20260525_182002` on HEAD `244e9fd`. Cascade computed and stamped a render plan: `[LFC] Sprint 6 render plan: mode=all, 6 line(s) (blocked=False, applied_max_n=6, arc_verdict=strong, cycle_count=0, 0 flat line(s) excluded)`. HuMo then ran but never emitted `[BatchHumoRender] Sprint 6 render plan ACTIVE: N line(s) selected ...` -- the log line is absent -- and rendered ALL 14 character lines (`b002 / b003 / b004 / b005 / b007 / b008 / b009 / b010 / b011 / b012 / b014 / b015 / b016 / b017`) instead of the 6 the plan selected. HuMo fell back to its pre-Sprint-6 render-all behaviour. PD1 (audio) clean -- mp4 landed.
- **Cause:** Root cause confirmed by 2026-05-25 parallel-agent grep across `_otr_freeze_cascade.py` + `batch_humo_render.py`. The cascade stamps `meta["render_plan"] = render_plan` in-memory at L748 of `_otr_freeze_cascade.py`. The cascade serializes the ledger to a JSON STRING returned via the `script_json` output socket but does NOT persist the post-stamp ledger to its on-disk path. `OTR_BatchHumoRender` is a separate node invocation that loads the ledger via `_load_ledger_with_path` from the `*_ledger.json` file on disk; that disk file was last written PRE-cascade (the writer's final save), so HuMo sees no `meta.render_plan` and the `render_plan_active` gate at `batch_humo_render.py:1680-1686` stays False -- skip filter never engages.
- **Fix:** FIXED 2026-05-25. Option B1 landed -- explicit `led.save()` after `_otr_freeze_cascade.py:748` so the post-cascade disk ledger carries `meta.render_plan`. The save is gated by `getattr(led, "save", None)` and wrapped in a try/except (PD1: a save failure logs a warning and HuMo falls back to render-all, never aborts the run) -- the `getattr` covers test fixtures that stub `led` as a SimpleNamespace without a `save` method (test_lfc_phase_7_8_readiness, test_lfc_g4_telemetry_derivation, etc.). Note: this fix relies on the BUG-273 refresh fix landing in the same commit -- without the refresh, the stamp lands on the stale orphaned dict and `led.save()` would serialize the LIVE dict without the stamp. The two bugs share a root cause (`Ledger.save()` rebind) so they ship together.
- **Verify:** New regression file `tests/test_freeze_cascade_meta_persistence.py` -- 3 BUG-274 tests via `_RebindingLedger` fixture (render_plan present on live meta post-cascade; render_plan captured in a save snapshot proving disk persistence; arc_verdict='uneven' lifts the blocking gate correctly). Full OTR suite 2853 passed / 21 skipped / 0 failed. Bug Bible 0 failed. LLM-slot sweep 23/23. Live-run validation pending: HuMo log must show `[BatchHumoRender] Sprint 6 render plan ACTIVE: N line(s) selected` and `(render_plan not_in_plan)` skip reason on excluded character lines.
- **Tags:** freeze-cascade, ledger-persistence, batch-humo, render-plan, sprint-6, sprint-7b, json-wiring, live-run, dict-rebind

### BUG-LOCAL-273: Cascade stamps meta.story_critic_report with 4 targets; reroll + render_plan both read empty [FIXED 2026-05-25]
- **Date:** 2026-05-25 | **Phase:** freeze cascade (Sprint 5B critic -> 5C reroll -> 6 render plan handoff) | **Bible candidate:** yes (root cause TBD)
- **Symptom:** Same live run (`signal_lost_bioluminescent_trench_descent_20260525_182002` / HEAD `244e9fd`). Sequence:
  1. `[OTR_StoryCritic] critic complete: arc_verdict=uneven, 0 continuity issue(s), 0 voice-drift note(s), 4 flat line(s), 4 reroll target(s), 13 line(s) in render priority` -- critic model has 4 reroll_targets.
  2. `[LFC] Sprint 5B story critic: arc_verdict=uneven, 4 reroll target(s), 4 flat line(s), 0 continuity issue(s), 13 line(s) in render priority (advisory -- no line text changed)` -- cascade's L685 log fires AFTER L683 stamps `meta["story_critic_report"] = story_critic_report.model_dump()`, reading the SAME model. Still 4 targets.
  3. `[OTR_Reroll] critic named no reroll targets -- nothing to do` -- the reroll module at `_otr_reroll.py:505-515` re-reads `meta.get("story_critic_report")`, runs `_coerce_report` (`StoryCriticReport.model_validate(raw)`), and sees an empty `report.reroll_targets`. No "did not validate" warning fires (would log at `_otr_reroll.py:434`), so coercion succeeded -- the dict it validated was already empty / target-less.
  4. `[LFC] Sprint 5C targeted reroll: verdict=no_reroll, 0 cycle(s), 0 line(s) re-composed`.
  5. `[LFC] Sprint 6 render plan: mode=all, 6 line(s) (blocked=False, applied_max_n=6, arc_verdict=strong, cycle_count=0, 0 flat line(s) excluded)` -- the render plan at L740 reads the SAME `meta["story_critic_report"]` and gets `arc_verdict=strong` (the `or "strong"` default in `_otr_render_plan.py:165`) + zero `flat_lines`. Render plan computed defaults.
- **Cause:** UNCONFIRMED. WRITE key (`_otr_freeze_cascade.py:683 -> meta["story_critic_report"]`) and READ keys (`_otr_reroll.py:505 -> meta.get("story_critic_report")`, `_otr_render_plan.py:162 -> meta.get("story_critic_report") or {}`) ALL agree on the same key -- naming is not the bug. Two candidates remain:
  - (A) Silent lossy pydantic round-trip: `StoryCriticReport(arc_verdict="uneven", reroll_targets=[RerollTarget x 4], flat_lines=[FlatLine x 4], ...).model_dump()` -> `StoryCriticReport.model_validate(dict)` should reconstruct losslessly, but a `model_config` setting (`extra='forbid'`, alias, `populate_by_name=False`, or a list-element validator) could silently drop entries. Pydantic v2 ordinarily round-trips, so this is the less-likely candidate -- but worth checking first because it would explain both the reroll and render-plan misses with a single defect.
  - (B) Between-stamp mutation: something between L683 (cascade stamp) and L505 (reroll read) replaces or empties `meta["story_critic_report"]`. The cascade docstring on `run_targeted_reroll` says it stamps `story_critic_report refreshed with the FINAL critic re-run`, but the no-reroll early-exit at L507-515 does NOT overwrite the stamp -- so the wipe is upstream of that path. Possible: a different code path between the cascade stamp and the reroll's `meta = ledger_data.setdefault("meta", {})` (L502) rebinds `ledger_data` to a different dict, OR the cascade re-loads the ledger from disk between L685 and L710.
- **Fix:** FIXED 2026-05-25. Confirmed root cause: candidate (B) -- between-stamp dict rebind. `Ledger.save()` in production_ledger.py:1012 rebinds `self.data` to a new merged dict on every save (BUG-LOCAL-108 merge-with-disk pattern). The Phase 1+2 reviewer at cascade L622 (`review_ledger`) calls `Ledger.save()` internally, detaching the cascade's `ledger_data` (bound at L570) and `meta` (bound at L576) from the live ledger. The Sprint 5B stamp at L683 then lands on an orphaned dict that downstream consumers never see -- the reroll, render_plan, and HuMo all re-read `led.data` and get the LIVE dict with no critic report. Pydantic round-trip is NOT lossy (candidate A ruled out): `StoryCriticReport.model_validate({})` succeeds because every field has a default (`arc_verdict="strong"`, empty lists), explaining the silent "no warning, all defaults" symptom. The fix adds two `ledger_data = led.data` + `meta = ledger_data.setdefault("meta", {})` refresh pairs in `nodes/_otr_freeze_cascade.py` -- one before the Sprint 5B critic stamp at L683, one before the Sprint 6 render_plan stamp at L740. Both stamps now land on the live post-save dict.
- **Verify:** New regression file `tests/test_freeze_cascade_meta_persistence.py` -- 5 passed (2 BUG-273 tests via a `_RebindingLedger` fixture that mimics production save's rebind, 3 BUG-274 tests sharing the same fixture). Full OTR suite 2853 passed / 21 skipped (baseline 2842 + 5 new + 6 transitively touched) / 0 failed. Bug Bible 23 passed / 1 skipped / 2 xfailed / 0 failed. LLM-slot sweep 23/23 tagged. Live-run validation pending the next operator episode -- expected log signal: `[OTR_Reroll] cycle 1/2: N reroll target(s)` (not "critic named no reroll targets") AND `[BatchHumoRender] Sprint 6 render plan ACTIVE: N line(s) selected`.
- **Tags:** freeze-cascade, story-critic, reroll, render-plan, ledger-persistence, sprint-5b, sprint-5c, sprint-7a, live-run, dict-rebind

### BUG-LOCAL-272: load_llm attention selector is dead code -- computed + logged but common_kwargs hardcodes sdpa [FIXED 2026-05-25]
- **Date:** 2026-05-25 | **Phase:** loader / VRAM (Sprint 4) | **Bible candidate:** no
- **Symptom:** No runtime fault on the current stack. `load_llm` (`nodes/_otr_model_loader.py`) resolves an attention-implementation selector `attn_impl` -- Flash Attention 2 when a `flash-attn` wheel is importable, else SDPA -- and logs the choice. But the `common_kwargs` dict passed to `AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained` hardcoded `attn_implementation="sdpa"`, so `attn_impl` was never consumed. On the Blackwell sm_120 / Windows / torch 2.10 stack this is benign -- no FA2 prebuilt wheel exists, so the resolved value is always `sdpa` and the hardcoded value matches -- but the selector is dead: if an FA2 wheel ever became importable, the load log would announce `flash_attention_2` while the model still loaded with SDPA. The Sprint 4 "attention selector logged on every load" requirement was also only half-met -- logged, but the log could silently diverge from the value actually used.
- **Cause:** `common_kwargs` was written with a literal `attn_implementation="sdpa"` instead of referencing the `attn_impl` variable the selector block computes a few lines above.
- **Fix:** FIXED 2026-05-25 (`nodes/_otr_model_loader.py`, Sprint 4 verify wave). `common_kwargs` now uses `attn_implementation=attn_impl` -- the selector is the single source of truth -- and an explicit `_runtime_log("Attention selector resolved: attn_implementation=...")` line fires on every load so the choice is auditable. No runtime behaviour change on the current stack (SDPA either way); fixes a future-FA2 false log and completes the Sprint 4 "logged on every load" requirement. Found by the Sprint 4 code-side verification -- no live run, and no test pinned the dead literal.
- **Verify:** `test_humo_tier_loader.py` 24 passed; `test_loader_body_profiles.py` / `test_loader_slot_primitives.py` / `test_meta_slot_transitions.py` 28 passed; full OTR suite 2787 passed / 21 skipped / 0 failed; Bug Bible 16 passed / 7 skipped / 3 xfailed.
- **Tags:** loader, vram, attention, flash-attention, sdpa, dead-code, sprint-4

### BUG-LOCAL-271: Cast auditor wrong_char_id violations go unrepaired -- expected=char_id but the repair resolves a name [FIXED 2026-05-25]
- **Date:** 2026-05-25 | **Phase:** writer / freeze-cascade cast auditor | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Live run `signal_lost_vances_promise_20260525_125401` (episode `pending_20260525_125025`, commit `1514e11`, 30-word smoke). The cast auditor (`audit_cast_contract:pre`) flagged ALL 5 lines with `wrong_char_id` violations (`audit complete: 5 violation(s), pass_clean=False`); `apply_deterministic_cast_repairs` resolved NONE: `wrong_char_id violation on line_id=bNNN suggested expected='c01'/'c02' but no cast member resolves; leaving row unrepaired for the Script Doctor` x5. The Script Doctor diagnosis then flagged 0 of them, so all 5 shipped unrepaired. Net effect on this episode was benign -- the 2 announcer lines carry `char_id='announcer'` and are handled downstream by BUG-079/BUG-129; the 3 character lines rendered fine on `c02` -- but the cast-contract `wrong_char_id` auto-repair is effectively dead.
- **Cause:** Contract mismatch. `apply_deterministic_cast_repairs` (`_otr_ledger_reviewer.py` ~L815-838, "Wiring-review #11" + Sprint 3F) treats `wrong_char_id.expected` as an auditor-suggested NAME -- it calls `_resolve_cast_member(v.expected, full_roster)` (exact case-fold, then Levenshtein <=3, against cast NAMES), then maps the resolved name to a canonical char_id via `char_id_by_name`. But the auditor emitted `expected='c01'` / `expected='c02'` -- a char_id, not a name. `_resolve_cast_member('c01', {'HAYES VANCE','ANNOUNCER'})` cannot match a char_id to a name -> `None` -> "no cast member resolves" -> row left unrepaired. Separately, the auditor (gemma-2-2b-it) flagged lines `b002/b003/b004` as `wrong_char_id` even though they already carry the correct `char_id=c02` -- small-model over-flagging, no longer confidence-gated since Sprint 3F removed the `confidence` field.
- **Fix:** FIXED 2026-05-25, commit `3e120df` (`nodes/_otr_ledger_reviewer.py`). Approach (b): the `wrong_char_id` repair branch now accepts a char_id. `apply_deterministic_cast_repairs` builds a case-fold `valid_char_ids` map from `cast_rows` (plus an `announcer` fallback) and validates `expected` against it, falling back to the legacy `_resolve_cast_member` name resolution only for a name-shaped `expected`. A line that already carries the resolved char_id counts as `repaired` -- no rewrite, no escalation -- which fixes the small-model over-flagging. `_AUDITOR_SYSTEM_PROMPT` updated: `wrong_char_id.expected` is documented as the correct char_id (`bad_casing`/`alias_used`/`invented_name` `expected` stays a NAME) and the auditor is told not to flag a line that already carries the correct char_id. Approach (b) over (a): the auditor only ever sees lines + the cast table keyed by char_id (`_render_lines_for_audit`/`_render_cast_contract_table`), never a speaker name. Full OTR suite 2787 passed / Bug Bible 16 passed, 0 failed. Bible promotion (Three-File Contract) pending the live-run verification below.
- **Verify:** a live episode whose `audit_cast_contract:pre` either reports 0 `wrong_char_id` violations on a clean cast, or reports them in the form the repair consumes and `apply_deterministic_cast_repairs` resolves them (`repaired` count > 0) instead of escalating all to the Script Doctor.
- **Tags:** writer, freeze-cascade, cast-auditor, wrong_char_id, contract-mismatch, sprint-3f, small-model-noise

### BUG-LOCAL-270: Fixed `seed` widget pinned the style picker's seed-flavor sampling [FIXED 2026-05-25]
- **Date:** 2026-05-25 | **Phase:** writer / style picker | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Twin of BUG-LOCAL-269, found by extending the same audit at Jeffrey's direction ("if a fixed seed is pinning anything to always be the same, that's not good -- no one is going to bother changing the seed"). The two-pass style picker's Pass-1 inventor samples 5 "seed flavors" from `_STYLE_PICKER_SEED_POOL` to ground its style invention; that sampling RNG (`picker_rng`) was `random.Random(int(seed))`, seeded from the `seed` widget. With the workflow's shipped `seed=42` (control `fixed`) the picker sampled the IDENTICAL 5 inspiration flavors on every episode -- so "let the story decide" was handed the same creative seed set each run.
- **Cause:** `OTR_LedgerScriptWriter.run()` built `picker_rng = _random.Random(int(seed))`. Same root cause as BUG-LOCAL-269 (cast) and BUG-LOCAL-260 (LEMMY cameo): a "random" selection seeded from a widget that ships a fixed value reproduces one outcome forever.
- **Fix:** New `_resolve_style_rng_seed()` (`OTR_LedgerScriptWriter.py`) draws a fresh OS-entropy 32-bit seed per episode for `picker_rng`; `run()` logs the seed. The `OTR_STYLE_SEED` environment variable forces a fixed seed for the C7 audio byte-identity regression. The same pass audited every remaining `seed`-widget consumer: the cast was already fixed (BUG-269); the news interpreter's `seed` is a cache-key + telemetry input (`news_interpreter.py` -- folded into the brief cache key and stamped to `brief.seed`), NOT an RNG, and is correct as-is; `seed_for_reviewer` named in the old `seed`-widget tooltip is a phantom -- it exists in no code (last referenced in the 2026-05-16 distillation design doc), so the tooltip claim was removed. The `seed` tooltip now states plainly that the seed drives no episode variety and can be left at the default; cast + style determinism for C7 is via the `OTR_CAST_SEED` + `OTR_STYLE_SEED` env vars.
- **Verify:** new `tests/test_style_randomization.py` -- 4 tests pinning `_resolve_style_rng_seed` (env override pins the seed; no env -> OS entropy in `[0, 2**32)`; 20 successive draws all distinct; blank/whitespace env falls back). Full OTR suite + Bug Bible green 2026-05-25.
- **Bible-candidate lesson:** same family as BUG-LOCAL-269 / 260 -- when a fixed-value seed widget is found pinning one "random" draw, audit EVERY consumer of that widget in the same pass; a fixed seed silently pins every downstream draw, not just the one a user happens to notice.
- **Tags:** writer, style-picker, rng, seed, determinism, c7, true-randomization, twin-of-269

### BUG-LOCAL-269: Fixed `seed` widget pinned the entire cast -- every episode rolled the identical characters [FIXED 2026-05-25]
- **Date:** 2026-05-25 | **Phase:** writer / cast lock | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Operator-reported -- the character "HAYES VANCE" appears in episode after episode; the cast never changes ("shows up in many stories -- rigged"). Confirmed by reproduction: with the workflow's shipped `seed=42` (`otr_scifi_16gb_full.json` node 1, control `fixed`), the open cast rolls `['HAYES VANCE', 'GULLIVER REEVES', 'JIMBO BLACK']` on EVERY run -- 5/5 identical episodes in a reproduction script; character #1 is HAYES VANCE for every `num_characters` value 1-6. The name pool holds ~110 first x ~50 last names (~5,500 combinations); with a fixed seed none but the seed-42 roll is ever produced. Corroborated by the existing log trail -- BUG-LOCAL-263 and BUG-LOCAL-267 both name `HAYES VANCE` as the character in unrelated May-24 episodes; the pinned cast had been silently shaping every episode and nobody had connected that it was the same character each time.
- **Cause:** `OTR_LedgerScriptWriter.run()` built the cast RNG as `cast_rng = _random.Random(int(seed))` -- seeded directly from the `seed` widget. `cast_pools.pick_first_last(rng, ...)` rolls `rng.choice(FIRST_NAMES)` + `rng.choice(LAST_NAMES)`; a fixed seed makes that roll deterministic, so a fixed `seed` widget reproduces ONE cast forever (announcer pick included -- `assemble_pre_locked_rows` consumes the same rng). Exact twin of BUG-LOCAL-260 (a fixed seed pinned LEMMY's ~11% cameo to 100%/0%): BUG-260's fix decoupled the LEMMY roll to OS entropy but explicitly LEFT cast names + announcer seed-deterministic for C7 byte-identity. This is that parked half, surfaced from the listener's side.
- **Fix:** Decoupled the cast RNG from the `seed` widget. New module-level helper `_resolve_cast_rng_seed()` (`OTR_LedgerScriptWriter.py`) returns a fresh OS-entropy 32-bit seed per episode (`random.SystemRandom().getrandbits(32)`); `run()` builds `cast_rng` from it and logs `cast RNG seed=N`. The `OTR_CAST_SEED` environment variable overrides it with a fixed integer -- the reproducibility path for the C7 audio byte-identity regression (set it in ComfyUI's environment before a baseline-capture / regression run; documented in `tests/test_audio_byte_identical.py`). The `seed` widget still drives the style picker + reviewer `seed_for_reviewer` derivation; its tooltip was corrected to drop the false "drives cast lock RNG (announcer voice + open-character name rolls)" claim. Mirrors BUG-260: random in production, explicit force path for C7.
- **Verify:** new `tests/test_cast_randomization.py` -- 4 tests pinning `_resolve_cast_rng_seed` (env override pins the seed; no env -> OS entropy in `[0, 2**32)`; 20 successive draws are all distinct; blank/whitespace env falls back to OS entropy). Full OTR suite + Bug Bible green 2026-05-25. C7 byte-identity: the operator C7 run now requires `OTR_CAST_SEED` set in ComfyUI's environment for a reproducible cast -- the `test_audio_byte_identical.py` structural tests are unaffected (the runtime byte-comparison is operator-gated behind `OTR_REGRESSION_RUNTIME`).
- **Bible-candidate lesson:** a "random" selection seeded from a widget that ships a fixed value is not random -- it reproduces one outcome forever. Twin of BUG-LOCAL-260. Any per-episode variety (cast, cameo, flavor) drawn from the C7 seed needs the same decoupling: OS entropy in production, an explicit env/force override for the byte-identity gate.
- **Tags:** writer, cast-lock, rng, seed, determinism, c7, true-randomization, twin-of-260

### BUG-LOCAL-268: json_str NameError silently kills run_story_brief_reflection's schema-repair arm [FIXED 2026-05-24]
- **Date:** 2026-05-24 | **Phase:** writer / story-brief reflection | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** No crash, no error line -- a silent functional failure. In `run_story_brief_reflection`, any LLM reflection output that parses as JSON but fails `StoryBriefModel` schema validation should trigger ONE repair pass at the clamped repair temperature (refinement section 3.4 / E-18). Instead every schema-invalid output collapses straight to `_failure_sentinel(REJECT_SCHEMA)` -- the repair pass never executes. The story brief degrades to the empty-string/failed sentinel far more often than the design intends, and the failure is invisible because the code path "succeeds" at returning a sentinel.
- **Cause:** `nodes/_otr_story_brief.py:648`, schema-validation repair arm. The call `_repair_pass(failed_output=json_str, rejection_reasons=..., technical_fn=..., ...)` references `json_str` -- a name never bound anywhere in `run_story_brief_reflection`'s scope (confirmed: `json_str` appears exactly once in the file, at L648). The raw LLM output is held in `raw`, bound at L604 (`raw = technical_fn(...)`). Evaluating the `failed_output=json_str` argument raises `NameError` BEFORE `_repair_pass` is entered; that `NameError` is caught by the broad `except (Exception, ValidationError) as exc2` at L657, which logs "schema validation failed after repair" and returns `_failure_sentinel(REJECT_SCHEMA)`. A coding typo is silently reclassified as a model-quality failure. The sibling content-validation repair arm at L679 passes `failed_output=brief_model.story_brief` correctly -- the defect is isolated to the schema arm.
- **Fix:** `json_str` -> `raw` at `_otr_story_brief.py:648`. One-word change. New regression test `tests/test_story_brief_repair_pass.py` forces a `StoryBriefModel` `ValidationError` on the first reflection output and asserts the schema-repair arm actually reaches `_repair_pass` (the test fails on the pre-fix code -- the repair arm dies in the broad `except` -- and passes post-fix).
- **Verify:** new `tests/test_story_brief_repair_pass.py` -- 4 passed 2026-05-24 (the spy-on-`_repair_pass` test would record zero calls on the pre-fix code -- the arm dies on the `NameError` before `_repair_pass` is entered). Regression green 2026-05-24: story-brief suite 107 passed / 4 skipped; `test_core.py` + `test_audio_byte_identical.py` + `test_meta_slot_transitions.py` + `test_writer_slot_routing.py` + `test_helper_paired_signatures.py` 89 passed / 2 skipped; Bug Bible regression 16 passed / 7 skipped / 3 xfailed -- 0 failed across every suite. (Story brief is a meta-only delta -- audio byte-identity is unaffected.)
- **Bible-candidate lesson:** a broad `except (Exception, ...)` wrapped around a fallback/repair arm silently converts a `NameError` (dead code) into a functional failure indistinguishable from a legitimate sentinel return. Any repair/fallback/retry arm needs a test that proves the arm actually executes -- not just that the enclosing function returns something. (Sibling pattern of BUG-LOCAL-213: comments promising behaviour must be exercised by an acceptance test in the same commit.)
- **Tags:** writer, story-brief, reflection, schema-repair, nameerror, silent-failure, broad-except, json-wiring

### BUG-LOCAL-267: ScriptDoctor rewrite re-injects the speaker label -- apply_doctor_edits bypasses strip_line_formatting [FIXED 2026-05-24]
- **Date:** 2026-05-24 | **Phase:** writer / freeze-cascade reviewer | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Episode `signal_lost_ozempics_glitch_20260524_174200` -- character dialogue lines carried the speaker name as literal spoken text. Ledger: b002 `text` AND `text_for_tts` = `"HAYES VANCE: I'm worried about what this could mean."`; b004 = `"HAYES VANCE not right."`. `text_for_tts` is `_clean_text_for_bark(text)`, which does not strip a speaker prefix, so Bark voiced the character's name aloud before the line. (Surfaced while diagnosing why that episode's HuMo output was near-static portrait stills -- the character barely "speaks" once the leaked labels and a hallucinated line are discounted.)
- **Cause:** The line composer cleans every composed line via `strip_line_formatting` (strips wrapping quotes, `[VOICE:]` tags, a leading `SPEAKER:` prefix, markdown) -- `compose_line` and both announcer passes route through it. But the Phase 3 ScriptDoctor reviewer runs AFTER composition, and `apply_doctor_edits` (`nodes/_otr_ledger_reviewer.py`) wrote a `rewrite` edit's `edit.payload` verbatim into `line["text"]` with no strip. `strip_line_formatting` is imported in exactly two files -- `OTR_LedgerScriptWriter.py` and `_otr_line_composer.py` -- not the reviewer. So when the doctor LLM emitted a rewrite payload with a `"HAYES VANCE:"` prefix, the label the composer had already stripped was re-injected. The ozempics run applied 3 doctor edits; b002 + b004 (both character lines -- the only role the doctor may edit) were doctor rewrites.
- **Fix:** `apply_doctor_edits` `rewrite` branch now routes `edit.payload` through `strip_line_formatting` (lazy import from `_otr_line_composer`, best-effort fallback to raw on import failure) before assigning `line["text"]`; `char_count` / `word_count` recomputed from the stripped text. The same strip the composer applies to its own output -- pure consistency, no behaviour change for a clean payload.
- **Verify:** regression -- `test_phase3_ledger_reviewer.py` + `test_core.py` + `test_audio_byte_identical.py` 109 passed / 1 skipped / 0 failed (the audio byte-identical proxy held -- the happy path carries no leaky doctor payload); Bug Bible 16 passed / 7 skipped / 3 xfailed / 0 failed. Fully confirmed when a real episode with a doctor rewrite shows a clean `text` / `text_for_tts` (no `"SPEAKER:"` prefix).
- **Known remaining gap:** the strip regexes (`_PREFIX_SPEAKER_COLON_RE`, `_build_named_prefix_re`) all require a separator (`:`, `-`, em-dash). A bare `"NAME "` prefix with no punctuation -- ozempics b004 `"HAYES VANCE not right."`, b001 `"ANNOUNCER strange..."` -- is not caught in ANY path. Closing it has a false-positive tradeoff (a line legitimately opening with the speaker's own name); parked in ROADMAP "SFX + CLEAN-LEDGER TRACK" workstream 2.
- **Tags:** writer, reviewer, script-doctor, freeze-cascade, speaker-label, text-hygiene, tts, bark, ledger, ozempics-glitch

### BUG-LOCAL-266: ComfyUI V3 AudioEncoderLoader returns a NodeOutput, not a tuple -- OTR_HuMoTierLoader crashes [FIXED 2026-05-24]
- **Date:** 2026-05-24 | **Phase:** video / HuMo load (OTR_HuMoTierLoader, node 72) | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** The BUG-LOCAL-265 verification run -- episode `pending_20260524_171500`, default workflow `otr_scifi_16gb_full.json`, tier `low_vram_default` -- crashed after 384.03 s. Audio, the FLUX radio still, and both FLUX character portraits all rendered clean; the run died the instant `OTR_HuMoTierLoader` (node 72) reached its audio-encoder load step:
  ```
  RuntimeError: OTR_HuMoTierLoader: node 'AudioEncoderLoader' returned
  <comfy_api.latest._io.NodeOutput object at 0x...>; expected a non-empty tuple.
    _otr_humo_tier_loader.py line 487  load -> _load_audio_encoder
    _otr_humo_tier_loader.py line 422  _load_audio_encoder -> _invoke
    _otr_humo_tier_loader.py line 391  _invoke -> raise RuntimeError
  ```
- **Cause:** ComfyUI 0.22.2 migrated the core `AudioEncoderLoader` node to its V3 node API. A V3 node's `FUNCTION` returns a `comfy_api` `NodeOutput` wrapper -- positional results stored on `.args`, exposed via the `.result` property (that tuple when non-empty, else None) -- NOT a bare tuple. `OTR_HuMoTierLoader._invoke` (shipped the same day in the BUG-265 Option C commit `09c2d49`) validated `isinstance(out, (tuple, list))` on every loader-node return and raised on anything else. The other nodes in the tier-loader chain -- `UNETLoader`, `ModelSamplingSD3`, `CLIPLoader`, `VAELoader` -- are still V1-style and returned plain tuples, which is why the run loaded the diffusion model, applied model sampling, and loaded CLIP + VAE successfully and crashed only at the audio encoder. A regression in the BUG-265 fix's new node, not a pre-existing defect. (The `missing audio encoder` / `unexpected audio encoder: decoder.layers.0-31 ...` lines just above the crash are benign: the audio encoder checkpoint is a full Whisper, whose `decoder.*` weights are `unexpected` because HuMo consumes only the Whisper encoder, and the `missing` mel-spectrogram buffers are non-persistent and regenerate at init. `AudioEncoderLoader` completed and returned successfully -- the warnings did not cause the crash.)
- **Fix:** `nodes/_otr_humo_tier_loader.py::HuMoTierLoader._invoke` -- before the tuple/list validation, when the `FUNCTION` return is not already a tuple/list, unwrap a V3 `NodeOutput` via its `.result` property to the positional-results tuple. Handles both V1 (tuple) and V3 (`NodeOutput`) core loader nodes; an empty `NodeOutput` (`.result` is None) still falls through to the loud `RuntimeError`. The `NodeOutput` shape was confirmed against ComfyUI v0.22.2 source `comfy_api/latest/_io.py` (`NodeOutput.__init__` stores `*args` on `.args`; the `result` property returns `self.args` when non-empty). Internal node-invocation plumbing only -- no INPUT_TYPES / widget / socket / output change on `OTR_HuMoTierLoader`, so `otr_scifi_16gb_full.json` node 72 is unaffected and needs no rewire. +4 regression tests `TestInvokeNodeOutputUnwrap` in `tests/test_humo_tier_loader.py` (V1 tuple pass-through, V3 NodeOutput unwrap, empty-NodeOutput still raises, unregistered-node still raises).
- **Verify:** regression green -- `tests/test_humo_tier_loader.py` 24 passed (incl. the 4 new), `tests/test_core.py` 59 passed, Bug Bible 16 passed / 7 skipped / 3 xfailed / 0 failed. Fully done when the operator re-runs the BUG-265 verification episode and `OTR_HuMoTierLoader` (node 72) loads the full HuMo stack past the audio encoder into `BatchHumoRender` Phase C. NOTE: this crash prevented the BUG-265 verification run from ever reaching the `PHASE-C-VRAM-PROBE`, so BUG-LOCAL-265 stays at pending-operator-probe posture until a clean run captures that line.
- **Tags:** video, humo, humo-tier-loader, comfyui-v3-api, nodeoutput, audio-encoder, comfyui-0.22, node-invocation, regression, json-wiring, bug-265-followup

### BUG-LOCAL-265: HuMo phase thrashes -- the OTR pipeline starves Phase C of VRAM [VERIFIED CLOSED 2026-05-26 -- post-NVIDIA-Studio-Driver-610.47 + Option C operator-verified end-to-end]
- **Date:** 2026-05-24 (logged) -> 2026-05-26 (verified closed) | **Phase:** video / HuMo render (BatchHumoRender Phase C) | **Bible candidate:** yes

**[VERIFIED CLOSED 2026-05-26]** Operator soak run
`signal_lost_spray_of_hope_20260526_162345` (HEAD `256f73c`,
HuMo-1.7B `low_vram_default` tier) on NVIDIA Studio Driver 610.47
(installed 2026-05-26) hit clean Phase C numbers:

  * `PHASE-C-VRAM-PROBE`: `torch allocated=25 MB reserved=128 MB`,
    `cuda free=14841 MB total=16303 MB`, `comfy-tracked models=0`.
    **Lever-1 reclaim (`free_otr_pipeline_residue`) works**: every
    upstream model unloaded cleanly before HuMo allocated -- the
    14.5 GB ceiling target hit exactly. No D3D Shared paging
    visible in the OS counters (the pre-driver baseline showed
    1098 MB on Shared during FLUX, and HuMo offloaded 10813 MB
    -- both gone).
  * Phase C s/it: **12-14 s/it** average over 20 steps. The
    pre-driver Option C bare-smoke baseline was 4 s/it on
    HuMo-1.7B, but that was with trivial inputs (6 steps, LoRA
    bypassed). On a real episode with proper 20 steps + cfg + real
    audio conditioning, 12-14 s/it is the realistic working
    baseline -- and it is NOT thrashing.
  * Episode shipped: full E2E pipeline ran through HuMo Phase C
    successfully, ledger persisted, .mp4 produced.

**Two factors closed the bug**:
  1. **BUG-LOCAL-265 Option C architecture** (shipped 2026-05-24,
     commit `OTR_HuMoTierLoader`): HuMo-1.7B as the default tier
     means HuMo's footprint fits the residual 14.5 GB headroom even
     before lever-1 reclaim. 17B tier remains opt-in via the
     `OTR_HuMoTierLoader` widget for operators with VRAM headroom.
  2. **NVIDIA Studio Driver 610.47** (installed 2026-05-26): release
     notes specifically call out ComfyUI model load/offload perf
     for larger workloads. The clean `PHASE-C-VRAM-PROBE` numbers
     suggest the driver's load/offload path closed the residual
     residue-after-`unload_all_models` window that BUG-LOCAL-231
     also flagged. Pre-driver baseline at the time this bug logged
     showed `loaded partially; 1744 MB usable ... 10813 MB
     offloaded`; post-driver shows `comfy-tracked models=0` BEFORE
     HuMo loads. The driver and the Option C architecture cooperate.

**Implications for BUG-LOCAL-231 + 244:** the same driver fix likely
moves those too. They stay open until a fresh post-driver FLUX
battery captures the new s/step baseline -- see
`reference_nvidia_studio_driver_610_47.md` recheck list.

----- ORIGINAL ENTRY (pre-close, retained for forensic audit) -----
- **Symptom:** In a normal OTR episode the HuMo render phase thrashes. `BatchHumoRender` Phase C: `Requested to load WAN21_HuMo` -> `loaded partially; 1744 MB usable, 1467 MB loaded, 10813 MB offloaded` -- ~88% of the model spilled to system RAM and streamed per sampler step. Pace 140-279 s/it (a clip took 3h43m in a prior soak); card pegged ~93% VRAM, ~80% system RAM.
- **Cause:** NOT the HuMo model. Bracketed 2026-05-24 with bare native-workflow smokes -- the stock official ComfyUI HuMo demo, zero OTR code (`workflows/humo_smoke_14b.json`, `humo_smoke_gguf.json`, `humo_smoke_q3.json`, `humo_smoke_1p7b.json`):
    - 14B fp8 bare: 46 s/it -- the same model OTR runs, ~6x faster outside the pipeline.
    - 17B Q3_K_M GGUF bare: 145 s/it -- loads smaller (only 2.1 GB offload) but pays a per-step dequant tax; net slower than fp8.
    - HuMo-1.7B fp16 bare: 4 s/it, 3.3 GB fully resident, zero offload -- rough at the smoke's 6 steps (LoRA bypassed) but acceptable at a proper ~20 steps + cfg.
  The 14B fp8 running clean bare proves the pipeline is the bottleneck: when Phase C loads HuMo, the OTR run still holds ~14 GB of VRAM in models loaded earlier in the pipeline (writer LLM, MusicGen, FLUX) via OTR's own loaders (`_otr_model_loader.py`) -- outside `comfy.model_management`, so `BatchHumoRender`'s inter-phase `unload_all_models()` cannot evict them, and HuMo gets ~1.7 GB of a 16 GB card. Same out-of-band-loader mechanism BUG-LOCAL-231 hypothesizes for the FLUX phase. (`BatchHumoRender`'s own BUG-LOCAL-081 comment already named the in-phase version: "~9.4 GB pinned before HuMo even starts to load.")
- **Fix:** pending -- VRAM-budget / model-choice change, round-robin gated per CLAUDE.md. Two live paths: **(A)** adopt HuMo-1.7B -- it fully fits the 16 GB card (3.3 GB, zero offload), renders at 4 s/it, and at proper settings (~20 steps + cfg, with real FLUX reference portraits) the output looked acceptable to the operator; sidesteps the VRAM problem outright. **(B)** keep the 14B/17B fp8 model (maximum quality) and free the pipeline VRAM residue before Phase C (lever 1). The A-vs-B choice is the round-robin call. Instrumentation landed this session: a `PHASE-C-VRAM-PROBE` log line in `nodes/batch_humo_render.py`, emitted right after the inter-phase cleanup -- reports torch allocated/reserved, real CUDA free/total, and the ComfyUI-tracked model list at Phase C entry. Additive logging, no behavior change (regression: `test_batch_humo_render` 42 passed, Bug Bible 23 passed). Next: capture the probe from a real episode to confirm what holds the ~14 GB, then round-robin the freeing fix.
- **Verify:** pending fix -- done when a normal OTR episode's `PHASE-C-VRAM-PROBE` shows HuMo getting a clean VRAM budget and the HuMo phase runs near the bare-smoke 46 s/it (14B fp8) instead of 140-279 s/it.
- **Resolution 2026-05-24 (Option C implemented):** Round-robin verdict / operator-accepted call -- ship HuMo-1.7B as the default, keep 17B opt-in, do the tiering upstream. Changes:
    - New node `OTR_HuMoTierLoader` (`nodes/_otr_humo_tier_loader.py`; registered `OTR_HuMoTierLoader` in `__init__.py`): one upstream loader for the whole HuMo stack with three tiers -- `low_vram_default` (HuMo-1.7B fp16, 20 steps, cfg 5.0, no distill LoRA -- the shipped default), `high_quality` (HuMo-17B/14B fp8, 6 steps, cfg 1.0, lightx2v 14B distill LoRA -- opt-in), `experimental_gguf` (HuMo-17B GGUF -- advanced only; GGUF demoted because its per-step dequant tax made it slower than fp8 in the bracket test). Emits MODEL/CLIP/VAE/AUDIO_ENCODER + the tier's steps/cfg. Tiering stays upstream so `BatchHumoRender` keeps its clean pre-loaded-inputs surface (no `model_id` widget; Prime Directive 6).
    - Hard auto-downgrade rule: when a high tier is selected but free VRAM after the residue clean is below `vram_safety_threshold_gb` (default 10 GB), the node either downgrades to HuMo-1.7B (`auto_downgrade` ON) or stops with a clear error -- a user never silently hits the 3h43m thrash path again.
    - Lever 1 -- `nodes/_otr_vram_levers.py::free_otr_pipeline_residue()`: drains the OTR-owned out-of-band caches `unload_all_models()` cannot see (writer LLM via `_otr_model_loader.unload_llm()`, Bark via `_otr_bark_lib._unload_bark()`) then runs the ComfyUI unload + CUDA flush. Called from `BatchHumoRender`'s inter-phase cleanup (replaced the bare `unload_all_models` + `soft_empty_cache`) and from `OTR_HuMoTierLoader` pre-load (so the auto-downgrade VRAM reading is post-clean). `OTR_UnloadAll` was also extended to drop the Bark cache.
    - `workflows/otr_scifi_16gb_full.json` rewired: the 6-node HuMo loader chain (UnetLoaderGGUF / LoraLoaderModelOnly / ModelSamplingSD3 / CLIPLoader / VAELoader / AudioEncoderLoader, ids 45-50) collapsed into one `OTR_HuMoTierLoader` (node 72) feeding `OTR_BatchHumoRender`; steps + cfg socket-driven from the tier; default tier `low_vram_default`. The FLUX->UnloadAll->HuMo gate (`flux_done_gate`) is preserved and extended -- `OTR_UnloadAll.unload_done` now also gates the tier loader.
    - The stale "~10-12 min/character line on RTX 5080" pre-batch estimate replaced with the 2026-05-24 bracket figure (~4:23/clip).
  Regression: full `tests/` walk 2617 passed / 21 skipped / 0 failed; Bug Bible 23 passed / 1 skipped / 2 xfailed; new `tests/test_humo_tier_loader.py` 20 passed; the workflow contract validator + canonical-baseline + live wiring-pin all green on the rewired JSON.
- **Verify -- partial:** the HuMo-1.7B default removes the user-facing thrash outright -- 1.7B is 3.3 GB, fully resident, zero offload, so it cannot thrash regardless of pipeline residue; the auto-downgrade rule guarantees the 17B opt-in never silently degrades. STILL PENDING an operator run: capture a real-episode `PHASE-C-VRAM-PROBE` line to confirm `free_otr_pipeline_residue` actually reclaims the ~14 GB residue (vs. the allocator holding reserved blocks). That number is relevant only to the 17B opt-in tier's viability, not to the shipped 1.7B default. Bug Bible promotion (Bible candidate: yes) is deferred until that operator probe lands.
- **Tags:** video, humo, batch-humo-render, humo-tier-loader, vram, vram-thrash, offload, phase-c, out-of-band-loader, lever-1, auto-downgrade, gguf, round-robin-gated, json-wiring, bug-231-related

### BUG-LOCAL-264: news_interpreter fails NewsBriefs schema with gemma-2-2b-it -- non-fatal, degrades announcer passes [LOGGED 2026-05-24 -- fix pending]
- **Date:** 2026-05-24 | **Phase:** writer / news interpreter | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** With `google/gemma-2-2b-it` in the technical slot, `news_interpreter` fails `NewsBriefs` schema validation on all 3 attempts, every run observed:
  - Episode `pending_20260524_132257`: `key_terms` -- "List should have at most 6 items after validation, not 10".
  - Episode `pending_20260524_135415`: TWO errors -- `script_brief` "String should have at most 350 characters" AND `key_terms` "not 9".
  Each time the node falls back to the raw `news_seed` ("falling back to raw news_seed for cast + outline"). Non-fatal -- the run continues -- but with no distilled `script_brief` / `news_close_brief`, the downstream announcer passes both drop to generic deterministic text: `[OTR_AnnouncerPass] intro: empty script_brief; using deterministic fallback`, same for the outro; `compose_flag_summary: {'announcer_intro_fallback': 1, 'announcer_outro_fallback': 1}`.
- **Cause:** gemma-2-2b-it overruns the `NewsBriefs` Pydantic caps (`key_terms` max 6, `script_brief` max 350). For `key_terms` the prompt DOES state the 2-6 range and the model ignores it -- a model-compliance miss. For `script_brief` the 350-char cap may or may not be stated in the prompt -- pending: the `news_interpreter` prompt builder needs checking to determine whether this is a prompt/schema contract gap (same class as BUG-263) or a pure compliance miss. Same overall family as BUG-263 -- small model vs strict schema -- but here the path has a graceful fallback so it never crashed; the cost is silent quality degradation rather than a dead run.
- **Fix:** pending -- lower priority than 261/263 because it is non-fatal. Same option family as BUG-263: coerce at the validation boundary (clamp `key_terms` to 6, truncate `script_brief` to 350), and/or state every cap explicitly in the news prompt, and/or relax the caps.
- **Verify:** pending fix -- done when an episode with `google/gemma-2-2b-it` either passes `NewsBriefs` validation or, if it still misses, the announcer intro/outro are news-driven rather than `*_fallback`.
- **Tags:** writer, news-interpreter, gemma-2, pydantic, schema-validation, string-too-long, llm-output-quality, announcer-fallback, bug-263-adjacent

### BUG-LOCAL-263: Casting prompt never told the model the description length cap the schema enforced -- run aborts [FIXED 2026-05-24]
- **Date:** 2026-05-24 | **Phase:** writer / cast lock + news interpreter | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Operator verification run for BUG-LOCAL-261 + 262. Episode `pending_20260524_132257`; `creative_model` AND `technical_model` both `google/gemma-2-2b-it`; `creativity='maximum chaos'` (temp 0.95 top_p 0.99); `num_characters=2`. Two distinct schema-validation overruns in one run:
  1. **FATAL** -- casting `HAYES VANCE` failed all 3 attempts; run aborted at 49.61 s:
     ```
     CastValidationLLMError: Casting failed for 'HAYES VANCE' after 3 attempts.
       Last error: schema validation failed -- 1 validation error for CastingResponse
         character_description
           String should have at most 200 characters [type=string_too_long]
       raised: nodes/_otr_casting.py:413 cast_one_character -> :639 lock_cast
            -> nodes/OTR_LedgerScriptWriter.py:1973 run
     ```
  2. **non-fatal (fallback fired)** -- `news_interpreter` failed all 3 attempts:
     ```
     news_interpreter FAILED: schema validation failed -- 1 validation error for NewsBriefs
       key_terms  List should have at most 6 items after validation, not 10
     -- falling back to raw news_seed for cast + outline (no key_terms enforcement)
     ```
     The fallback let the run continue past the news pass; it died later at casting.
- **Cause:** Two root causes, one per overrun. (1) FATAL casting -- a prompt/schema contract gap, NOT a model-size failure. The casting prompt builder (`_otr_casting._build_user_prompt`) only ever showed the model the placeholder `"<short>"` for `character_description`; it never stated a character count. Meanwhile `CastingResponse.character_description` hard-enforced `max_length=200`. Any model writing a normal 1-2 sentence description (~220-280 chars) overran a limit it was never told, and the 3-attempt retry kept failing identically. The prior models (`gemma-4-E4B-it`, Mistral-Nemo) just happened to write shorter on average and got lucky against the under-specified prompt; gemma-2-2b-it did not -- a latent code defect surfaced by the model change. (2) non-fatal news -- `news_interpreter`'s prompt DOES state the 2-6 `key_terms` range and gemma-2-2b-it ignored it (returned 10): a model-compliance miss, but that path has a graceful raw-`news_seed` fallback so it did not kill the run. This is NOT a JSON-parse failure: BUG-LOCAL-261's parser consolidation held this run (zero `Extra data` errors) -- the failure is one stage downstream, at Pydantic validation of the already-parsed object.
- **Fix:** 2026-05-24, per Jeffrey's call ("don't be so pushy about length limits"). The FATAL casting half is fixed in `nodes/_otr_casting.py`, two changes: (1) `CastingResponse.character_description` `max_length` raised 200 -> 750 -- a normal 1-2 sentence description now fits with wide margin; 750 is a runaway guard, not a content target. The old 200 was NOT load-bearing: `_format_prior_entry` already trims the echoed description to 60 chars before it re-enters any downstream prompt, so the stored length never touches prompt budget / `context_cap`. (2) the casting prompt placeholder `"<short>"` -> `"<vivid, 1-2 sentences>"` in both `_build_user_prompt` and its docstring example, closing the prompt/schema contract gap so the model gets real guidance. Internal schema + prompt only -- no node `INPUT_TYPES` / widget / socket change, no workflow-JSON rewiring. The non-fatal `news_interpreter.key_terms` 10-vs-6 overrun was deliberately NOT changed here -- that prompt already states the 2-6 range and the path has a graceful fallback; left as a separate lower-priority follow-up.
- **Verify:** regression 2026-05-24 via the venv pytest -- `tests/test_otr_casting.py` 57 passed / 0 failed (the `CastingResponse` schema tests cover valid / bad-gender / short-description / gender-case; none pinned the 200 upper bound, so raising it broke nothing), Bug Bible regression 23 passed / 1 skipped / 2 xfailed (baseline held). The lone `test_core.py::test_node_types_otr_or_known` red is the pre-existing parked `UnetLoaderGGUF` workflow-allowlist gap (handoff + BUG-261 adjacent-issue note) -- unrelated to this change, which touches only a pydantic `Field` cap and two prompt strings. **Operator-verified 2026-05-24:** episode `pending_20260524_135415` (`creativity='maximum chaos'`, `google/gemma-2-2b-it` in both slots, `num_characters=1`) cast `HAYES VANCE` on attempt 1/3 -> `voice=v2/en_speaker_9 gender=male`, cast lock completed (2 rows), zero `CastValidationLLMError`. The writer then ran clean end-to-end -- outline, line composition, announcer passes, title regen, DONE. [FIXED]
- **Tags:** writer, casting, news-interpreter, gemma-2, pydantic, schema-validation, string-too-long, llm-output-quality, bug-261-adjacent, bug-262-adjacent

### BUG-LOCAL-262: Style picker aborts the run on Gemma-2 -- its chat template hard-rejects the system role [FIXED 2026-05-24 -- operator-verified]
- **Date:** 2026-05-24 | **Phase:** writer / style picker | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** An episode run aborted in the style picker. The technical-slot model was `google/gemma-2-2b-it`. Console trace:
  ```
  StyleGenerationFailedError: chooser LLM call failed: TemplateError: System role not supported
    nodes/_otr_style_picker.py _run_chooser
      -> nodes/OTR_LedgerScriptWriter.py generate_fn (~line 616)
        -> tokenizer.apply_chat_template
  ```
  The chooser pass (Pass 2 of the two-pass style picker) builds a message list `[{"role":"system",...},{"role":"user",...}]` and the writer's `generate_fn` passes it straight into `tokenizer.apply_chat_template`. Gemma-2's Jinja chat template contains a literal `raise_exception("System role not supported")`, so the render throws `TemplateError` and the run halts.
- **Cause:** Gemma-2 has no system role by design -- its chat template hard-rejects it. The OTR writer generate path (`OTR_LedgerScriptWriter._build_truncating_generate_fn -> generate_fn`) and the polish path (`_otr_model_loader.make_generate_fn` / `make_polish_generate_fn`) passed message lists containing a `{"role":"system",...}` entry directly into `apply_chat_template` with no per-tokenizer guard. `gemma-4-E4B-it` and Mistral-Nemo accept a system role, which is why this never surfaced before -- the bug was latent until a Gemma-2-family model was used in the technical slot.
- **Fix:** System-role normalization at the message-encoding boundary. New shared helpers in `nodes/_otr_loader_backends.py` (exported in `__all__`): `tokenizer_supports_system_role(tokenizer)` renders a minimal 2-message probe through `apply_chat_template(tokenize=False)` and returns `False` if it raises; `normalize_messages_for_tokenizer(tokenizer, messages)` is a no-op when the system role is supported, otherwise folds each system message's content into the first following user turn (`system_text + "\n\n" + user_text`) and drops the system entries -- the standard Gemma pattern. Edge cases handled: orphan system message with no following user turn is converted to a user message; multiple system messages concatenated in order. The probe result is cached once per model residency (closure-cell in the writer's `_build_truncating_generate_fn`, mirroring the `_min_p_unsupported` idiom; `cache_entry["_system_role_supported"]` in the loader). Every live `apply_chat_template` call site is routed through the normalizer: `OTR_LedgerScriptWriter.py` `generate_fn`, `_otr_model_loader.py` `make_generate_fn` + `make_polish_generate_fn`, and `visual/llm_polish.py` `_build_prompt`. `encode_messages_for_row`'s `transformers_default` branch is also routed so the dispatch path and the live path do not diverge. A `google/gemma-2-2b-it` catalog row was added (`transformers_safetensors`, `context_window=8192`, `gated_terms`/`research_lane` -- Gemma-2 ships under the restricted Gemma Terms of Use, NOT Apache 2.0; only Gemma 4 is Apache 2.0) with its `docs/model-license-google--gemma-2-2b-it.md` audit file and an audit-targets entry. Internal generate-path plumbing only -- no node `INPUT_TYPES` / widget / socket change, no workflow-JSON rewiring.
- **Follow-up (NOT fixed here -- separate architectural call):** The live writer generate path (`OTR_LedgerScriptWriter.generate_fn`) calls `tokenizer.apply_chat_template` directly and bypasses the `_otr_loader_backends.encode_messages_for_row` dispatch infrastructure entirely. Re-routing the writer through `encode_messages_for_row` (so a row's `chat_template_kind` drives encoding end-to-end) is a separate, deliberate architectural change and was intentionally NOT done in this fix. This change makes both paths consistent for system-role handling; it does not unify them.
- **Verify:** New `tests/test_system_role_fold.py` (11 tests): a fake system-rejecting tokenizer, after `normalize_messages_for_tokenizer`, receives a list with no system role and the system text prepended to the first user message; a no-op assertion for a permissive tokenizer; multi-system concat, orphan-system conversion, assistant-turn passthrough, input-not-mutated, `encode_messages_for_row` folding, exported-symbol, and a `gemma-2-2b-it` clean-pick assertion. Suites green: `test_system_role_fold.py` 11 passed, `test_chat_template_kind_dispatch.py` 7 passed, `test_catalog_matches_audit_files.py` 1 passed, `test_model_catalog_schema.py` 5 passed, `test_audio_byte_identical.py` all pass (audio path byte-identical, untouched), Bug Bible regression 23 passed / 1 skipped / 2 xfailed. `py_compile` clean on all six touched `.py` files. The lone `test_core.py::test_node_types_otr_or_known` failure is the pre-existing, already-logged `UnetLoaderGGUF` workflow-allowlist gap (see BUG-LOCAL-261 adjacent-issue note) -- unrelated to this change. **Operator-verified 2026-05-24:** episode `pending_20260524_132257` ran `google/gemma-2-2b-it` in both model slots; the two-pass style picker cleared cleanly (`inventor attempt 1/3 OK` -> `chooser picked 'mission_narrative_space'`, pass1=1857ms pass2=453ms), zero `TemplateError`, zero system-role rejection. The chooser is a technical-slot pass and `news_interpreter` also ran through gemma-2-2b -- the system-role fold is verified across multiple call sites in one run. [FIXED]
- **Tags:** writer, style-picker, chat-template, gemma-2, system-role, llm-slot, json-wiring

### BUG-LOCAL-261: Casting phase aborts the run when the LLM emits a second JSON object after the cast object [FIXED 2026-05-24]
- **Date:** 2026-05-24 | **Phase:** writer / cast lock | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Episode `pending_20260524_004323` aborted in `nodes/_otr_casting.py` while casting the character `HAYES VANCE`. All 3 cast attempts failed identically with `json.loads failed: JSONDecodeError('Extra data: line 2 column 1 (char 144)')`; the run then raised `CastValidationLLMError` from `OTR_LedgerScriptWriter.py:1958` (`lock_cast`) -- `Casting failed for 'HAYES VANCE' after 3 attempts`. No audio, no video -- the writer never finished cast lock. Run config: `creative_model=google/gemma-4-E4B-it`, `creativity='maximum chaos'` (temp 0.95), `num_characters=1`.
- **Cause:** `nodes/_otr_casting.py::_extract_json_block` extracted JSON by slicing from the first `{` to the LAST `}`. When the casting LLM emits two top-level objects -- `{...}{...}` -- that slice returns BOTH concatenated, and the strict `json.loads()` that followed rejected the trailing object as `Extra data`. The high sampling temperature (0.95, "maximum chaos") makes a hallucinated second object likely; the brittle extractor is the actual defect.
